# Clocklings code review prompts 501–600

This standalone Markdown file is part 6 of 8 and contains 100 English review prompts. Start at 501 and process the supplied prompts through 600. If only one block is supplied, process that block. Use only the numbered scope supplied for this run.

Use the current working tree at C:\Dev\Clocklings. Read the current repository instructions and PROJECT.md. Before the first edit, inspect the branch and run git status --short. Capture the starting hash of PROJECT.md and a private, content-aware baseline for existing staged, unstaged and relevant untracked work. Build the path and evidence inventory this part needs from the current repository. Use the current part's ledger as the record of the checks performed in this run. Results from another part are optional evidence when their scope and revision can be verified.

Work through the numbered headings in order. Read the matching fenced block for each prompt and keep a Finnish ledger by prompt number. Record the paths inspected, evidence, confirmed findings, minimal changes, validation commands and actual outcomes. Record zero-change and blocked results as well. Give an uncovered contract relevant to this part an S-prefixed supplemental ID. Read the file in small ranges if needed. If interrupted, report the last fully completed ID, the unfinished or next ID, the existing changes and the validation completed. Resume from that boundary when asked to continue.

After completing 600, reconcile this part's ledger, inspect review-owned changes against the starting baseline and report the verification performed for this part. Work on another part begins when it is supplied.

## Contents

- [501–540 Languages and audio](#languages-and-audio)
- [541–583 Build and release verification](#build-and-release-verification)
- [584–600 End-to-end journeys and test evidence](#end-to-end-journeys-and-test-evidence)

First re-read the repository-root PROJECT.md for this review item. Use it to understand product purpose, intended behavior, architecture, constraints, supported platforms and deliberate decisions. Leave PROJECT.md untouched throughout this review unless the owner explicitly requests its modification in a separate task.

Compare an afternoon/evening transition from actual formatter ranges, with the same input used for all visible and spoken labels.

Use the actual German formatter, its language fixtures, the corresponding resources and Texts at the affected surface. Record the raw minute value and the exact user-visible or spoken string under review.

Base every finding on the actual current repository and verify the full relevant code path, configuration, tests, version constraints, and existing safeguards before reporting or changing anything. It is completely acceptable to find no issue and make no change. Do not invent findings, force a quota, treat a preference as a defect, or modify code merely to produce visible work. Do not over-engineer, add speculative abstractions, or refactor code that is already correct, safe, clear, and fit for purpose. Prefer the smallest adequate solution. Less is better.

Follow reachable callers, inputs, state ownership, readers and writers through to the observable result. Check actual SDK/library versions, build variants, guards, validations, error paths, lifecycle and existing tests. Explain how a failure occurs or which established requirement is violated. Classify confirmed defects, unverified risks, style preferences and alternatives separately. Assign severity from concrete impact. Performance, leak, concurrency and security claims require a specific mechanism. If evidence is insufficient, record the gap and leave the code alone.

Inspect first. If and only if a real issue is confirmed, implement the smallest safe correction necessary to address its root cause. If no issue is confirmed, make no change. Do not perform opportunistic cleanup, unrelated refactoring, dependency updates, broad rewrites, or speculative improvements.

Preserve intended behavior, public interfaces, data formats, storage schema, UI and compatibility except where the confirmed defect requires a change. Fix a shared root cause once at its actual owner. Recheck a case already addressed by an earlier edit and mark it resolved only after verification. Avoid renames, file moves, helpers, layers and frameworks for their own sake. Architectural, dependency, migration, build, schema or public API changes require direct technical necessity and appropriate authorization. Review every changed file individually. Do not run broad automatic fixes.

Work only in C:\Dev\Clocklings. Before editing, inspect the branch and run git status --short. Preserve existing staged, unstaged and untracked user work. Before the first review edit, preserve a content-aware baseline of pre-existing work and the starting hash of PROJECT.md. Reuse it throughout the run to distinguish review edits from prior edits. Use only an explicitly identified dedicated Clocklings emulator for device work. Restore owned settings and stop any temporary processes or capture sessions you start. Reserve broader checks for the end of the run. Leave owner-run lc and sc to the owner. Report exact commands, outcomes and any environmental block.
